same channels as the allegation?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. She wants all private academy records published.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. She hopes the academy will cancel the competition.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. She wants Cassian to become a judge.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. Mara considered asking for a refund of the ceremony.","\"I imagined marble,\" she said.","Cassian held the door while a box slid down a leaning stack and landed at his feet. Its label announced IMPORTANT RESEARCH, DO NOT DROP.","\"The room appears to have anticipated your expectations.\"","The archivist was packing material for transfer to an external store. A repair to the roof had been delayed, rain had reached the north wall, and every dry shelf was full. Fellowship access did not stop a building leaking. It did, fortunately, permit Mara to request the technical records associated with her object before they left.","The archivist checked the number. \"Four boxes. One missing.\"","\"Which one?\"","\"The one people usually want.\"","Mara put down her new tool case. \"An efficient filing principle.\"","The surviving boxes contained maintenance notes, correspondence, photographs, and a paper manual with a broken spine. At first she searched for Elena Vale. Nothing appeared. She tried the hospital destination code, the name of the theater, and the abbreviation Cassian had recognized. That brought a small collection of references, most pointing to the missing box.","She could feel the morning slipping away. Somewhere in the academy, people were still congratulating her. Here, the prize had become a table, a lamp, and the possibility of arriving one move too late.","Cassian set a glass of water beside her. \"You're reading faster than you're turning the pages.\"","\"A useful new skill.\"","\"Mara.\"","She stopped. The sound of her name, without an argument attached, reached her more effectively than advice would have. She drank the water and began again.","The first discovery came from an invoice. An old equipment bureau had supplied material for a series of mobile demonstrations. The theater's loan had been part of the same project. Its machinery carried images and voices, but the research notes concerned what volunteers recalled afterward. A trial had begun with ordinary tasks: recognizing a tune, placing events in order, describing a familiar room.","Mara turned the page and saw a little flower in the margin.","Five petals. One petal folded inward. A tiny line at the stem where her mother always pressed too hard.","For several seconds she could not read anything else.","Cassian followed her gaze. He did not ask whether she was sure. He had seen the silver flower often enough to know what it meant to her. When she moved the paper closer to the lamp, he adjusted the shade without speaking.","The note beside the drawing was signed E. Vale, technical consultant. It referred to a replacement spring in the traveling display and a concern about how the demonstration had been described to volunteers. A printed instruction said that recipients should be told the procedure was designed to improve recall. Elena had crossed out \"improve\" and written, \"Test. Improvement has not been established.\"","Mara laughed once, painfully. \"She used to correct advertisements on the bus.\"","\"Did they deserve it?\"","\"Usually. She carried a pencil for emergencies.\"","Once, when Mara was small, Elena had brought home a clock belonging to a woman who could pay only with vegetables. Mara had complained that they already had enough onions. Her mother had asked whether the woman ought to live without a clock until someone richer needed a repair.","\"We could charge for the onions,\" Mara had suggested.","Elena had laughed so hard that the little spring escaped her tweezers. They had spent an hour looking for it under the kitchen table. Mara remembered her mother's bare feet, the dust on her knees, and the ridiculous triumph when they found it beside a carrot.","She had forgotten that afternoon for years. Now a pencil flower brought it back entire.","There she was, in an untidy margin: not a secret portrait, not a grand title, but a woman unable to leave a misleading sentence alone. Mara had spent the week wondering where her own persistence came from. The answer lay on cheap paper, beside a complaint about a spring.","The next page was a recruitment draft. Volunteers would attend repeated sessions. It offered a modest payment and transport from community institutions. Elena had marked the line authorizing later use of the recordings. \"Separate choice,\" she had written. \"Not a condition of payment.\"","A second reader had replied in a different hand: \"Too many forms discourage participation.\"","Elena's answer occupied the width of the margin. \"Then explain them better.\"","Mara could almost hear the voice. She wanted, with an intensity that made breathing difficult, to turn and find her mother at the end of the table, annoyed about the damp and asking whether anyone had brought lunch.","There was only an empty chair.","The archivist returned with a trolley. The boxes were due downstairs. Mara asked for copies of the signed notes and the recruitment draft, then noticed that the folder's page numbers jumped. The index listed an appendix on follow-up transport. Its tab remained. The pages were gone.","\"It may be in the transfer box,\" the archivist said. \"If it was misfiled, you'll have to request it from the external store.\"","A uniformed porter was already wheeling that box towards the service lift.","Mara was out of her chair before the archivist finished. She caught the lift door with her shoulder, apologized to a startled cleaner, and stepped inside beside the trolley. Cassian arrived a breath later, carrying her coat and the authorization sheet she had forgotten.","\"A predictable logistical weakness,\" he said, handing it over.","\"You could sound less pleased.\"","\"I could.\"","The porter checked the sheet and agreed to wait while they inspected the folder at the loading desk. Outside, rain was blowing under the awning. A man tried to cover three boxes with one sheet of plastic while a truck driver made increasingly inventive comments about the weather. Mara crouched beside the trolley and opened the clasp.","The appendix was there, wedged between a census summary and a proposed grant budget. Its top sheet carried no names, only recipient codes and destinations. One code matched the missing catalogue entry. The destination was Mercy House, the hospital in the old theater district. A later note added storage rooms at the theater behind it.","Cassian leaned down. His sleeve brushed her shoulder. \"The inventory was right.\"","Mara found a second sheet beneath the first. It listed transport arrangements for participants who required follow-up. She did not read the covered names. The date column was visible, along with the initials of the person authorizing each move. The final entry bore her mother's initials.","Its filing date came after the date Mara had been told Elena died.","The rain seemed to fall without sound.","\"That's after,\" she whispered.","Cassian steadied the edge of the folder as the wind caught it. \"Let's photograph the headings as well.\"","She looked up at him, almost angry. Then she looked where he pointed. The printed field said RECEIVED BY ARCHIVE. Beside it, a separate field held the date the document had been written. That date was earlier. The brief, impossible hope did not vanish cleanly. It left a hollow place behind it.","Mara sat back on her heels. \"I wanted it to mean something else.\"","\"I know.\"","He knelt beside her in the rain-blown loading bay, unconcerned about his trousers. She leaned into his shoulder for one breath, then another. No one asked for a statement. No one offered her a conclusion she could not bear to distrust. His warmth was an ordinary fact, and for the moment it was enough.","The porter cleared his throat gently. He could give them another five minutes.","Mara wiped her face with the heel of her hand, annoyed to discover that not all the wet came from the weather. She found the authorization note and asked for supervised copies of the pages relevant to the equipment. The participant sheet would need a separate request, with Noor involved in protecting the people it named.","The porter called the archivist. Permission came through with three minutes to spare. Cassian held the plastic cover against the rain while Mara checked every page. She did not let the disappointment turn her careless.","Back upstairs, the archivist gave her the copies and an index reference for the originals. Elena's notes would remain available through the fellowship. The copies smelled of warm toner. Mara slid them into the tool case, where the medal had been sitting since the ceremony.","Holt found them at the exit. She read the destination code and frowned. The academy had donated equipment to Mercy House years earlier. The hospital was now in administration. Records could be dispersed with its assets if no one knew which boxes mattered.","\"Can the fellowship inspect the objects there?\" Mara asked.","\"It can propose a project. The authority to enter belongs to the administrator.\"","Mara looked at the little flower in her mother's margin. For years it had been a memory she carried alone. Now it pointed towards a hospital, a theater, and people whose stories had been packed under somebody else's numbers.","\"Then,\" she said, \"let's make a proposal they can't afford to ignore.\""],"questions":[{"type":"reference","stem":"What causes Mara to revise her first interpretation of the late date on the document?","options":["The document contains a verified recent message from Elena.","Cassian identifies the initials as belonging to an unrelated person.","The archivist admits the date was invented during the competition.","The date records archive receipt; a separate field shows the document was written earlier."],"answer":3,"explanation":"她最初把较晚日期理解成母亲后来仍在工作，但表头显示那是归档收到日期；另一栏的成文日期更早，不能据此确认母亲生存。","evidence":[50],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1533,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0023\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">卷二｜谎言学院</div><h1>Her Mother&#x27;s Margins</h1><p id=\"chapter-0023-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3204\" data-words=\"3204\">fellowship</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4648\" data-words=\"4648\">key</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5733\" data-words=\"5733\">opened</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2534\" data-words=\"2534\">door</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2054\" data-words=\"2054\">cupboard</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3239\" data-words=\"3239\">finally</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7157\" data-words=\"7157\">room</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8383\" data-words=\"8383\">that</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7665\" data-words=\"7665\">smelled</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9189\" data-words=\"9189\">wet</a> cardboard. It gave her three pages to list possible sources of funding.","She read both sections, crossed out the forty-word box, and attached a photograph of the damaged theater screen. On the back she wrote: \"Objects have owners. Before disposing of one, find out who is missing the other.\"","Holt considered it over breakfast. \"That isn't a budget.\"","\"I have one of those too. It is profoundly less attractive.\"","The budget allowed for a limited inventory, safe storage, and an examination of the surviving technical records. It did not promise to renovate a hospital for the price of a college grant. Noor added a separate request concerning participants who might need follow-up. Cassian supplied the references connecting the missing-person inquiry to the equipment transfers. Ada organized the documents so a stranger could find the important page without knowing any of them.","By eleven, the proposal had three signatures and one large obstacle. The interim administrator was willing to permit entry if the public trust approved the survey. The trust's chairman was attending a charitable garden reception. His secretary said he would be unavailable until the following week.","The equipment auction was scheduled for Monday.","Mara looked at the invitation on Holt's desk. Fellows had been invited to attend the reception as examples of the academy's excellent judgment. For once, she was happy to be useful publicity.","\"I'll need my dress,\" she said.","Cassian glanced at the folder in her hands. \"This is going to be an unusual garden party.\"","\"Only if he makes me chase him through a hedge.\"","She wore the dark dress Mrs. Wren had lent her and fastened its clasp with the little feather-shaped pin. The new tool case did not fit discreetly beneath her arm, so Cassian carried it. He declined to explain the case to anyone at the reception, which made it considerably more interesting than the jeweled bags on the surrounding tables.","The chairman, Mr. Voss, was listening to a speech about generosity when Mara found him. He greeted her as the fellowship winner, congratulated Cassian on his good fortune, and asked whether they had chosen a wedding venue.","\"We are considering a building with serious structural problems,\" Mara said. \"May I show you the relevant proposal?\"","Cassian made a sound into his glass. Voss stared. By the time he realized that the building was a hospital, Mara had placed the survey summary in his hand.","\"This needs a proper meeting,\" he said.","\"The auction is on Monday. Your secretary offered next Thursday.\"","\"Then perhaps the inventory can be done afterward.\"","\"If the records are sold with the equipment, afterward may mean trying to find twenty buyers in six countries. You can authorize a limited survey now without approving a single purchase. The administrator has already agreed to the conditions.\"","Voss looked towards the champagne table. Mara stepped a little to the side, leaving him a route through the garden but not an easy way to pretend the conversation had ended. Her shoes pinched. She wished she had worn the boots.","\"You are persistent,\" he said.","\"The academy has just given me a medal for it. I thought I should make good use of the investment.\"","A woman behind him laughed softly. Selene Ardent stood beneath a pale umbrella, immaculate in a suit whose fabric caught the light like water. Mara recognized her before she spoke. Around them, people subtly adjusted their positions to include her in the conversation.","\"A limited survey sounds entirely reasonable,\" Selene said. \"Although I wonder whether it is the best use of a gifted young conservator.\"","Voss looked relieved to have the question taken out of his hands. Mara kept her attention on Selene.","\"What use did you have in mind?\"","\"A private collection. Excellent facilities. A salary that would allow you to concentrate on your work instead of worrying about the next invoice. I could arrange an introduction.\"","It was a very good offer. Mara disliked how quickly she could imagine the studio: reliable light, clean tools, no landlord knocking at the door. Selene had chosen the temptation carefully. Nothing in her tone suggested a bargain about a missing page or an old hospital. The pressure lay in how easy the alternative sounded.","\"I'd be delighted to hear about paid work,\" Mara said. \"After the survey.\"","\"The hospital will disappoint you. Places like that consume every resource offered to them.\"","\"Then a collection of silver cups should be easier to leave waiting.\"","Selene's smile did not move. \"You may discover that glamour is more useful than indignation.\"","Mara smiled back. \"I'm wearing a very good dress. I thought I might manage both.\"","Cassian lowered his glass. It was a tiny movement, but Mara knew he was trying not to laugh. Selene noticed it too. Her gaze passed over the tool case in his hand and returned to Mara.","\"I see you have considerable support.\"","\"He carries the heavy things,\" Mara said. \"I make the difficult requests. We've found the division quite effective.\"","Voss cleared his throat. The request was still in his hand, and Selene had already called it reasonable in front of six attentive guests. There was suddenly no elegant way to explain why it should wait. He asked for a pen.","Mara produced one before Cassian could.","The authorization was limited to specified buildings, objects, and records. The hospital administrator would control access to clinical areas. Patient information would remain protected, with Noor handling any properly authorized clinical follow-up. The trust could approve a short pause in the equipment disposal while the survey took place. Voss signed, then tried to hand the paper to Cassian.","\"It's her project,\" Cassian said.","The paper came to Mara. Her name sat under the grant reference in plain black ink. She did not need to imagine a crown to enjoy the moment.","They left before the next speech. In the carriage entrance, she looked down at the signature and let herself grin.","\"You nearly laughed,\" she accused Cassian.","\"You threatened to investigate a wedding venue. I consider my restraint exceptional.\"","\"I was sincere about the structural problems.\"","\"I expect the chairman will remember them whenever someone mentions marriage.\"","At Vey House, the kitchen became a temporary planning room. June arrived with two notebooks, Ivo with a revised van schedule, and Rowan with the expression of a man who had found an expensive lie in a cheap folder. Ada made space at the table by moving a decorative bowl Cassian said nobody had ever eaten from.","Rowan read the authorization. \"No ownership. No unlimited account. No permission to make promises on behalf of the trust. Good. You'd be surprised how often those distinctions cost money.\"","\"Can you look at the administration accounts?\" Mara asked.","\"If the administrator releases them. I'll start with the public contracts.\"","June wanted to photograph the first visit. Noor said the patients were not a backdrop. They settled on recording the building and the inventory process, with individual consent for any personal interviews. Ivo asked whether the hospital had a basement. Cassian asked whether he meant a workshop. Ivo said he meant a dry basement, a condition people consistently failed to appreciate.","Ada remained after the plans had been divided. \"If you need someone to make sense of the registry, I can do that. But I can't keep volunteering until the theater decides whether I exist.\"","Mara pulled the approved budget towards them. There was a small allowance for cataloguing. She marked the hours they could afford and turned the page so Ada could read it.","\"A short contract,\" she said. \"Proper records. No promise of work we can't pay for.\"","Ada checked the figures, crossed out a task that would take twice the time allowed, and wrote a better estimate beside it. Then she signed. Neither woman thanked the other for a rescue. They had agreed on a job.","The hospital's administrator sent over a key that evening. Attached to it were three unpaid bills and a note directing them to Sister Agnes, the ward supervisor. A porter also delivered a small parcel marked FRAGILE. It had been left among the academy's items for transfer to Mercy House, and somebody hoped the new survey team would save the cost of a separate delivery.","\"A promising start,\" Rowan said. \"One key, three debts, and a mystery box.\"","Mara set the parcel beside her mother's copied notes. The medal lay in her tool case. She had imagined winning would mean a door opening onto a cleaner, easier life. Instead, it had given her permission to walk into a place everybody else wanted to close.","She found she preferred the door.","Later, when the others had gone, Cassian returned the empty teapot to the kitchen. Mara stood by the window, trying to decide whether the last line in Elena's note was an instruction or an impatient question. She shut the folder before the words could blur.","\"Tomorrow,\" she said.","He glanced at the key in her hand. \"Mercy House?\"","\"Yes. I hope you're still available for carrying heavy things.\"","\"My current employer has made no attempt to dismiss me.\"","She held out the key. He placed his hand beneath hers, leaving it between them. The gesture was warmer than a promise and easier to believe.","\"You can drive,\" she said. \"I'll decide which door we try first.\""],"questions":[{"type":"scope","stem":"What does the signed Move patients whenever they think it would help.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. Take ownership of Mercy House and spend unrestricted trust funds.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The House That Bit the Duke","volume":"3","anchor":"chapter-0025","paragraphs":["The hospital tried to bite Mara before she reached its front door. A loose brass letter dropped from the arch, missed her head, and struck Cassian's shoulder with a solemn bang. He caught it before it hit the ground. The letter was an enormous H. \"A greeting,\" he said. \"The house has recognized your importance.\" Mara examined the broken screw. \"It has recognized the availability of a wealthy hostage. We ought to negotiate before it acquires a taste for gold.\"","Their investigation order covered the bankrupt hospital and the decades-old theatre behind it. It did not grant ownership, unlimited money, or permission to smuggle patients away. At hand were three unpaid bills, one key, and a parcel marked FRAGILE. The parcel contained a plaster angel with a pistol painted on its sleeve. \"Our former collector was eccentric,\" said Sister Agnes, the ward supervisor. \"He thought goodness needed security.\"","She led them through a wide hall where a cloudy mirror reflected the arrival of several elderly residents. A nylon curtain divided the ward from a former classroom. Behind it, an elderly man was trying to eat breakfast while a young attendant attempted to remove his tray. \"My diabetes does not mean I must consume a dreadful grey formula,\" he protested. \"I used to dine at the finest inn in this vicinity. That pot contains neither beef nor hope.\"","\"The beef is in the kitchen,\" Agnes said. \"The pot contains onion soup. And your medication comes after food.\" She lowered her voice. \"The old supplier stopped delivery. Today's meal has no additive because today's meal has almost no ingredients.\" An empty vitamin bottle stood beside a tablet computer displaying a rising temperature warning. The refrigerator timer had failed overnight.","Noor arrived carrying a thermometer and a loaf. She asked to analyse the food temperature data before anyone announced an epidemic. \"Poorly stored food can bring about illness. That does not demonstrate that every unhappy patient has an infection.\" A freelance recorder had already called the situation an imminent catastrophe. June persuaded him to reflect on the difference between recording distress and helping it flourish.","An alarmist notice near the hatch seemed to allude to a latent danger in every meal. Mara asked the chemist who supplied the ward whether its warning could derive from reliable data. He could identify three discrete concerns, namely storage, delivery, and cost. They affected poor residents disproportionately. \"Fairness means affordable food,\" he said. \"We attach importance to each calorie because a missed meal can become an extreme risk.\" His practical interpretation made the notice look radically less informative. Mara asked June not to let the foundation use her as its mouthpiece. They could tackle the actual failures without attempting to saturate the press with fear. \"Okay,\" June said. \"I care about the headline. I also care about the people beneath it.\"","An antagonistic former administrator blocked the kitchen. He called the residents troublesome and described their fear as hysteria. Mara's impression of him could only deteriorate. \"Emotion isn't a defect,\" she said. \"And a complaint about food isn't meaningless merely because the person making it is poor.\" Cassian moved to the doorway, leaving her room to confront the man without his shadow swallowing the argument.","The administrator claimed that the hospital had an optimum system: a global corporation supplied food, medicine, and equipment under one formal contract. A non-profit foundation paid the invoices. \"Very nicely arranged,\" Rowan said, turning a page. \"The foundation owns the merchant, the merchant owns the supplier, and the supplier charges the foundation twice the reasonably available price. Sustainable development appears to mean that their profit can thrive forever.\"","\"Competition produces variation,\" the man replied stiffly. \"A monopoly protects quality.\" Rowan gave the conceited guy a grin. \"A self-contradictory statement. How sought-after.\" Mara made him repeat the exact names of the competing contractors. One was phony; another had no known location. The third was registered near a lake on the equator and sold fishery equipment. No one could explain its speciality in biomedical refrigeration.","In the ward, the elderly man introduced himself as Gideon Pike, retired theatre manager and occasional terror of the back row. \"My granny taught me to complain before I learned to sleep,\" he said. \"Age has allowed me to augment the skill.\" He asked if Cassian's castle had a buffet. Cassian admitted it had a kitchen. \"A basic defect in your résumé,\" Gideon replied. \"You should diversify.\"","Noor checked Gideon's blood sugar and asked what he could tolerate. He wanted butter, cream, and brandy. She offered soup, bread, and water. \"Plenty of people eat healthy meals without becoming unhappy,\" she said. \"I have no idea how they behave afterward,\" Gideon answered. A bowl arrived with a cheesy smile drawn in pepper. He declared it amazing, then demanded a smaller spoon. Disability had weakened the muscle in his right hand; it had left his razor-sharp mind in excellent working order.","Mara fetched the spoon from a cupboard. A quartz clock above it had stopped at six. Next to it, a sci-fi bestseller promised to revive the brain through a single chromosome treatment from a pharma promoter. Someone had underlined CURE. Noor closed the book. \"A neurologist can investigate a memory problem. A physiologist can study how the body functions. Neither title permits its owner to promise a cure for anything whatsoever.\"","A visiting psychiatrist had found no reason to dismiss Gideon's account of missing hours; a psychologist had offered ways to work through his anxiety. His therapist taught him how to hold a cup again. Each practitioner had different evidence and a different task. Their uncertainty was understandable. It did not make his suffering imaginary. \"People who suffer deserve honest limits,\" Noor said. \"We must spare no effort to find out what happened. That is different from pretending we already know.\"","At noon, the dentist downstairs reported a lack of alcohol disinfectant. The surgeon had postponed a routine procedure because a sterile kit was incomplete. A bloodied dressing in the wrong bin revealed another failure. Mara wanted to start with every problem at once. \"A fine way to end up with three disasters,\" Agnes said. \"Pay attention to the fridge first. The rest of the house can take turns.\"","Ivo found a hole in the cooling pipe. Someone had tried to bore through a wall and struck it. The repair would restrict kitchen access until dusk. In a split-second calculation, Mara compared the repair price with the day's food bill. The savings would outweigh the cost within a week. A small gain could matter when inflationary prices could impoverish a ward before its next payment. She signed; Ivo climbed a ladder and muttered something about the popularity of unnecessary holes.","The administrator returned with a predatory offer. His firm would inject cash immediately if they signed over the theatre. Mara felt a prick of temptation. The theatre might be worth restoring, but an empty stage could not feed a patient. Rowan slid the contract beneath the projector. Its secondary clause allowed the firm to shift future losses onto the residents. \"You want to shift the bill onto people whose rent is already overdue,\" Mara said. \"Your refreshment voucher is a very small token beside that.\"","\"I am offering certainty.\"","\"You are offering to make us uncertain about a different catastrophe.\" She refused the trap. Her refusal would probably annoy several creditors, and she had no plan grand enough to overshadow that fact. Frankly, she was fearful. Cassian watched her hand linger above the unsigned page. \"Would you like advice,\" he asked, \"or someone to stand beside you while you decide?\" The question made it difficult to remember why the man was so inconvenient.","\"The second. For now.\"","He nodded. It was a reservation she could trust.","They called a meeting in the theatre. Its curtain needed fibre repairs, and a former farmer had used the storage room for straw. A donor proposed a gold tower for the entrance. Mara asked whether the gold could be compared to a refrigerator in usefulness. \"That depends on your passion for cold soup,\" Gideon called from his wheelchair. The crowd laughed. Cassian looked solemn until a joyful child asked whether he intended to play the donkey in the Christmas rehearsal.","\"Only if I can learn to do the ears properly,\" he replied.","The donor complained that such informality would heighten individualism and reduce respect. Mara asked him to contemplate the wall behind him. Water had begun to penetrate the plaster; shrinkage had opened a seam beside the stage. They could not let the building stagnate until its collapse became inevitable. Nor could they evacuate every resident simply to celebrate a prosperous investor's new development. Their fear was a contributory cause of resistance, not an excuse to silence them.","Agnes became chairperson of a temporary residents' committee. She would restrict no one's right to speak, but asked each person to summarize a proposal. A young nurse suggested round-the-clock meal delivery from local kitchens. A frustrated cook offered a second-hand stove. A high performer from a Wall Street bank offered a complicated fund in the form of a loan. Rowan, whose identity had impressed everyone until he began washing bowls, asked the cook for her number first.","The hospital's faded magazine described its birthplace as a public institution founded by a twin sister and brother. That commonality with the theatre mattered: both had once shared a service yard. In the midst of the meeting, Gideon pointed to an old subtitle painted beneath the stage crest: A HOUSE FOR ALL. \"Think of a house as a promise,\" he said. \"It is only a marvel if it keeps the weather out.\"","The sentence caught Mara harder than any dramatic sentiment. Her mother had taught her that restoration meant discovering what an object needed, rather than making it mirror the restorer's vanity. She had been obsessed with an impossible search for a single missing woman. Here were forty-three people whose loss was present, visible, and still happening. One search did not excuse neglect of the other.","Cassian found her testing a doorway with her shoulder. A stubborn hinge resisted. \"May I assist?\" he asked. \"You may. No drastic intervention.\" Together they eased it open. A warm kitchen smell drifted through: onion, beef, and bread. For the first time that day, Mara's hands stopped shaking. The work was enormous, and she was weary. The house was unhealthy, the accounts mixed, and the committee was unlikely to agree on everything. None of that made the first meal silly.","At dusk, Ivo switched on the repaired refrigerator. The indicator began to glow. Gideon raised a cup of water, with reluctance, to the theatre's new residents. Cassian offered Mara the last piece of bread. \"You have plaster on your nose.\" She reached for it. \"Other side.\" His mouth moved suspiciously. \"Are you laughing at me?\" \"Repeatedly.\" She stole the bread before he could change his mind.","Then Agnes brought an old patient-transfer ledger. Six admissions came from the same port clinic, although the forms gave six different referring doctors. Beside every name was a black square and the same measured field: oxygen capacity. Gideon's appetite had returned; his memory had not. Mara set the ledger between herself and Cassian. Somewhere beyond the hospital wall, a warehouse siren began its evening call."],"questions":[{"type":"Inference / motive","stem":"Why does Mara reject the administrator's offer of immediate cash?","options":["It offers no actual financial assistance.","Its contract would expose the residents to future losses.","It would oblige her to seek advice from Cassian.","It would prevent any restoration of the theatre."],"answer":1,"explanation":"合同的附加条款把未来损失转嫁给住户，所以她拒绝的是具体交易条件。文中承认对方提供现金，并未否认所有私人资金，也未说剧院必然无法修复。","evidence":[16,18],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1887,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0025\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">3</div><h1>1.
